- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10558v1)
- **Authors**: Peiying Zhang, Nanxuan Zhao, Jing Liao
- **Abstract**: Scalable Vector Graphics (SVGs) are highly favored by designers due to their resolution independence and well-organized layer structure. Although existing text-to-vector (T2V) generation methods can create SVGs from text prompts, they often overlook an important need in practical applications: style customization, which is vital for producing a collection of vector graphics with consistent visual appearance and coherent aesthetics. Extending existing T2V methods for style customization poses certain challenges. Optimization-based T2V models can utilize the priors of text-to-image (T2I) models for customization, but struggle with maintaining structural regularity. On the other hand, feed-forward T2V models can ensure structural regularity, yet they encounter difficulties in disentangling content and style due to limited SVG training data. To address these challenges, we propose a novel two-stage style customization pipeline for SVG generation, making use of the advantages of both feed-forward T2V models and T2I image priors. In the first stage, we train a T2V diffusion model with a path-level representation to ensure the structural regularity of SVGs while preserving diverse expressive capabilities. In the second stage, we customize the T2V diffusion model to different styles by distilling customized T2I models. By integrating these techniques, our pipeline can generate high-quality and diverse SVGs in custom styles based on text prompts in an efficient feed-forward manner. The effectiveness of our method has been validated through extensive experiments. The project page is https://customsvg.github.io.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a novel two-stage pipeline for style customization in text-to-vector (T2V) graphics generation.  The first stage trains a T2V diffusion model using path-level representations to ensure structural regularity in the generated SVGs. The second stage distills styles from customized text-to-image (T2I) models to enable style customization of the T2V model. This is achieved by fine-tuning T2I models on style examples and then using the output images as augmented data for training the T2V model.  The method aims to leverage the strengths of both feed-forward T2V models (for structural regularity) and T2I models (for style priors) to create high-quality, diverse SVGs in custom styles based on text prompts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its two-stage approach to style customization for T2V generation. Combining a T2V diffusion model focused on structural integrity with style distillation from T2I models is a unique contribution. While existing T2V methods exist, they often lack robust style control, or struggle to balance style transfer with structural coherence. The idea of using T2I models as style guidance for T2V is interesting. Prior works using T2I have focused primarily on optimization based approaches, thus a feed-forward model is a good contribution.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practically important problem in vector graphics design: creating collections of vector graphics with consistent visual appearances, which is vital for branding and user interfaces. By providing a method for generating stylized SVGs from text, the paper contributes to making the design process more efficient and accessible. Furthermore, the idea of disentangling content and style at the level of vector graphics, is inherently important. The experiments do indeed show a clear benefit over competing methods. The inclusion of a human evaluation is also useful for verifying the impact of the proposed method.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing T2V methods regarding style customization.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The two-stage pipeline is a creative and effective way to combine the strengths of different model architectures.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The quantitative and qualitative results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method compared to existing baselines. The user study further validates the perceptual quality of the generated SVGs.
    *   **Detailed implementation**: The paper provides enough detail on the implementation for the work to be reproducible.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dataset Dependency:** The initial T2V model is trained on the FIGR-8-SVG dataset, which may limit its ability to understand and generate SVGs with more complex content or class labels. This is explicitly acknowledged by the authors.
    *   **Style complexity**: The generated examples from the style are less consistent in situations where more fine-grained stylistic detail is present, as shown by the "flower" example.
    *   **Compute intensiveness**: The need to maintain and train an external T2I model can increase the complexity of style incorporation.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a significant advancement in the field of T2V graphics generation. The two-stage pipeline effectively addresses the challenge of style customization while maintaining structural regularity. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of the proposed method. Although the paper has some limitations regarding dataset dependency and complexity of styles, the novelty and potential impact are significant.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper is a strong contribution to the field. The two-stage structure is clever, and results are promising. The score is not higher because the method still needs high-quality labelled data and the resulting performance is tied to an external T2I model, placing some upper bounds on performance/effectiveness. Additionally, although the results are promising, there is still much further to improve the fidelity and applicability of vector graphics generation, and this score reflects this factor. The limitations of the datasets on which the models are trained and the complexities of certain styles provide opportunities for future work.

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Neural Thermodynamic Laws for Large Language Model Training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10559v1)
- **Authors**: Ziming Liu, Yizhou Liu, Jeff Gore, Max Tegmark
- **Abstract**: Beyond neural scaling laws, little is known about the laws underlying large language models (LLMs). We introduce Neural Thermodynamic Laws (NTL) -- a new framework that offers fresh insights into LLM training dynamics. On the theoretical side, we demonstrate that key thermodynamic quantities (e.g., temperature, entropy, heat capacity, thermal conduction) and classical thermodynamic principles (e.g., the three laws of thermodynamics and the equipartition theorem) naturally emerge under river-valley loss landscape assumptions. On the practical side, this scientific perspective yields intuitive guidelines for designing learning rate schedules.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary of the paper and a critical evaluation with a score.

**Summary:**

The paper "Neural Thermodynamic Laws for Large Language Model Training" introduces a novel framework, called Neural Thermodynamic Laws (NTL), to understand and optimize the training dynamics of Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors draw parallels between the training process of LLMs (particularly within the "river-valley" loss landscape) and classical thermodynamics. They show how key thermodynamic quantities (temperature, entropy, heat capacity) and principles (laws of thermodynamics, equipartition theorem) naturally emerge in LLM training.  They introduce a simplified, analytically solvable toy model of the river-valley landscape and use it to derive practical guidelines for designing learning rate schedules, especially focusing on the warmup-stable-decay (WSD) approach. The paper emphasizes the importance of learning rate η as an effective temperature and analyzes the effects of fast dynamics on slow dynamics through entropic forces. Experimental results on GPT-2 validate the theoretical insights, showing that the derived learning rate schedules lead to improved performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's main strength lies in its novel application of thermodynamic principles to the understanding of LLM training. While connections between neural networks and thermodynamics have been explored before, this paper is the first to explicitly draw those connections *specifically within the context of the river-valley loss landscape*, which is characteristic of LLMs. The introduction of the analytically solvable toy model and its connection to LLM behavior are also novel. Deriving an optimal learning rate schedule from thermodynamic principles is a significant contribution. The idea of interpreting learning rate as temperature in the context of LLM training offers fresh insights.

**Significance:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the LLM training field. Currently, many optimization techniques in LLMs are based on empirical observations.  Providing a *principled*, *theoretically-backed* approach to understanding training dynamics is highly valuable. If the NTL framework proves robust and generalizable, it could lead to more efficient and effective training strategies for LLMs. The analysis of entropic forces and their influence on training is also potentially significant, as it offers a new perspective on the interactions between different parameter groups during training. The paper also offers practical insights in the form of an optimal learning rate decay schedule.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong Theoretical Foundation:** The paper successfully bridges the gap between LLM training and thermodynamics, providing a solid theoretical framework.
*   **Analytically Solvable Model:** The toy model allows for rigorous analysis and provides intuitive insights.
*   **Practical Implications:** The derived learning rate schedules offer actionable guidelines for practitioners.
*   **Experimental Validation:**  The GPT-2 experiments provide empirical support for the theoretical claims.
*   **Clear and Concise Writing:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow, despite its technical nature.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Simplifications and Assumptions:** The toy model makes several simplifying assumptions (e.g., quadratic loss landscape, isotropic conditions) that may not hold true in complex LLMs. While the authors acknowledge these limitations, it is crucial to evaluate the robustness of their conclusions under more realistic conditions. This is a typical problem for theoretical works, as simplifying assumptions are required to make progress.
*   **Limited Experimental Scope:** The experiments are primarily based on a GPT-2 small model. More extensive experiments on larger models and datasets would be necessary to demonstrate the generalizability of the results. The analysis is a bit toyish and limited to idealized conditions.
*   **Lack of Direct Comparison to Existing Optimization Methods:** A more direct comparison to state-of-the-art LLM optimization techniques would strengthen the paper. Demonstrating that NTL-inspired methods outperform existing methods would provide more compelling evidence of its value.
*   **Physicist's Style**: Some derivations and approximations rely heavily on intuition and physical arguments, which might lack mathematical rigor. This might be a problem for some members of the ML community.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

*   Encouraging further research on the application of thermodynamic principles to LLM training.
*   Inspiring the development of new optimization algorithms based on the NTL framework.
*   Providing a more systematic approach to designing learning rate schedules.

**Justification of Score:**

Despite its weaknesses, the paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field. The theoretical framework is well-developed, and the practical implications are promising.  While the experimental scope is limited, the results provide initial support for the theoretical claims. The paper's potential impact on the field warrants a high score. However, more work is needed to validate the framework under more realistic conditions and demonstrate its superiority over existing methods. Given the novelty, potential significance, and theoretical contributions, a score of 7 seems reasonable. It's a solid contribution, but not without its limitations that future research must address.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### End-to-End Vision Tokenizer Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10562v1)
- **Authors**: Wenxuan Wang, Fan Zhang, Yufeng Cui, Haiwen Diao, Zhuoyan Luo, Huchuan Lu, Jing Liu, Xinlong Wang
- **Abstract**: Existing vision tokenization isolates the optimization of vision tokenizers from downstream training, implicitly assuming the visual tokens can generalize well across various tasks, e.g., image generation and visual question answering. The vision tokenizer optimized for low-level reconstruction is agnostic to downstream tasks requiring varied representations and semantics. This decoupled paradigm introduces a critical misalignment: The loss of the vision tokenization can be the representation bottleneck for target tasks. For example, errors in tokenizing text in a given image lead to poor results when recognizing or generating them. To address this, we propose ETT, an end-to-end vision tokenizer tuning approach that enables joint optimization between vision tokenization and target autoregressive tasks. Unlike prior autoregressive models that use only discrete indices from a frozen vision tokenizer, ETT leverages the visual embeddings of the tokenizer codebook, and optimizes the vision tokenizers end-to-end with both reconstruction and caption objectives. ETT can be seamlessly integrated into existing training pipelines with minimal architecture modifications. Our ETT is simple to implement and integrate, without the need to adjust the original codebooks or architectures of the employed large language models. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our proposed end-to-end vision tokenizer tuning unlocks significant performance gains, i.e., 2-6% for multimodal understanding and visual generation tasks compared to frozen tokenizer baselines, while preserving the original reconstruction capability. We hope this very simple and strong method can empower multimodal foundation models besides image generation and understanding.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ETT (End-to-End Vision Tokenizer Tuning), a method for jointly optimizing vision tokenizers and downstream autoregressive tasks.  The key idea is to address the misalignment between vision tokenizers (typically trained for low-level reconstruction) and the needs of downstream tasks (e.g., image captioning, VQA) that require higher-level semantic understanding. ETT leverages the visual embeddings of the tokenizer's codebook and optimizes the tokenizer with a combination of reconstruction and captioning losses.  The authors demonstrate that ETT improves performance on multimodal understanding and visual generation tasks compared to baselines that use frozen vision tokenizers, while preserving the original reconstruction capabilities of the tokenizer.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the recognition of the limitation of decoupled vision tokenization and downstream training, and the proposed solution of end-to-end tuning.  While end-to-end training is not a new concept, its application to jointly optimize vision tokenizers *with* downstream tasks in the autoregressive multimodal learning paradigm is relatively novel. Specifically, bridging discrete token representations with codebook embeddings allows gradient flow, enabling the end-to-end training. This is a significant improvement over previous works that freeze the vision tokenizer, thereby limiting downstream task performance.

**Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its practical impact on multimodal foundation models. The reported performance gains (2-6%) on multimodal understanding and visual generation tasks are meaningful. The method is also claimed to be simple to implement and integrate, making it a potentially valuable tool for improving existing and future multimodal models. Furthermore, the demonstrated ability to preserve the original reconstruction capability of the tokenizer is crucial, ensuring that the benefits for downstream tasks don't come at the expense of image generation quality.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies a practical limitation in the existing paradigm of multimodal learning.
*   **Effective Solution:** The proposed ETT is a simple and effective approach to addressing the problem. The concept is easy to understand and implement.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The authors provide extensive experimental results demonstrating the benefits of ETT across various tasks and datasets.
*   **Preservation of Reconstruction Quality:** A key strength is maintaining the original tokenizer's reconstruction quality while improving downstream tasks.
*   **Codebook Embeddings:** The idea of utilizing the codebook embeddings, instead of only the discrete indices, is vital for enabling end-to-end training and leveraging the tokenizer's richer representation.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalization Discussion:** While the paper shows improvements across several datasets, a more in-depth analysis of the types of tasks or data distributions where ETT is *most* effective would be beneficial.  It might not be universally superior and understanding when ETT provides the most value is important.
*   **Overclaims on Impact:** The paper states the hope that ETT can empower multimodal foundation models "besides image generation and understanding."  The results presented are almost entirely in these two areas and there is little discussion on other potential applications (e.g., embodied AI, robotics). This statement is an overreach.
*   **Lack of Deeper Analysis:** While ablation studies are conducted, a more thorough analysis of *why* ETT works so well would strengthen the paper.  For example, visualizing the learned codebook embeddings or analyzing the gradient flow during training might offer valuable insights. The current analysis is somewhat superficial.
*   **Incremental Contribution:** While novel, the core mechanism leverages existing methods (end-to-end training, tokenization). The innovation is primarily in combining and adapting these for a specific problem, which may lower the overall impact.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence research and development in multimodal foundation models by shifting the focus from isolated tokenizer training to joint optimization. It may encourage researchers to explore more sophisticated methods for aligning vision tokenizers with downstream task requirements.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a clear and practical solution to a relevant problem in the field of multimodal learning. The end-to-end approach, demonstrated with compelling empirical results, significantly increases downstream tasks while holding its original representation power. However, there is limited analysis into the approach and over claims about the method's broad scope. Moreover, the general method has more of an incremental contribution to an approach that blends and adapts existing techniques for a specific application. Taking into account the strengths, weaknesses and novelty, a score of 7 is warranted.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### 3D-Fixup: Advancing Photo Editing with 3D Priors
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10566v1)
- **Authors**: Yen-Chi Cheng, Krishna Kumar Singh, Jae Shin Yoon, Alex Schwing, Liangyan Gui, Matheus Gadelha, Paul Guerrero, Nanxuan Zhao
- **Abstract**: Despite significant advances in modeling image priors via diffusion models, 3D-aware image editing remains challenging, in part because the object is only specified via a single image. To tackle this challenge, we propose 3D-Fixup, a new framework for editing 2D images guided by learned 3D priors. The framework supports difficult editing situations such as object translation and 3D rotation. To achieve this, we leverage a training-based approach that harnesses the generative power of diffusion models. As video data naturally encodes real-world physical dynamics, we turn to video data for generating training data pairs, i.e., a source and a target frame. Rather than relying solely on a single trained model to infer transformations between source and target frames, we incorporate 3D guidance from an Image-to-3D model, which bridges this challenging task by explicitly projecting 2D information into 3D space. We design a data generation pipeline to ensure high-quality 3D guidance throughout training. Results show that by integrating these 3D priors, 3D-Fixup effectively supports complex, identity coherent 3D-aware edits, achieving high-quality results and advancing the application of diffusion models in realistic image manipulation. The code is provided at https://3dfixup.github.io/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "3D-Fixup: Advancing Photo Editing with 3D Priors" introduces a novel framework for 3D-aware image editing.  The key idea is to leverage 3D priors to guide 2D image edits, specifically focusing on edits like object rotation and translation, which are challenging for traditional 2D-based editing methods. The approach uses a feed-forward deep learning model trained on a dataset generated from real-world videos. The data generation pipeline reconstructs 3D meshes from video frames using existing image-to-3D techniques, estimates 3D transformations between frames, and then renders the transformed meshes to create guidance images. These guidance images, combined with the original image, are used to condition a diffusion model to generate the final edited image.  The authors demonstrate qualitative and quantitative improvements over existing 2D and 3D image editing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Strengths:** The primary novelty lies in the integration of real-world video data, 3D priors obtained from image-to-3D reconstruction, and a diffusion model for 3D-aware editing. The automated data generation pipeline is a significant contribution, enabling the creation of a training dataset without manual 3D annotations. Using video to capture real-world dynamics (lighting, shadows) is a clever approach. Framing the problem as "3D-fixup" of an initial 2D reconstruction and leveraging that signal into a generative process appears novel. The use of cross-attention to maintain identity details from the source image while adapting to the 3D guidance signal is also a positive contribution.
*   **Weaknesses:** Some components are built upon existing techniques (diffusion models, image-to-3D). While the *integration* of these components is novel, the individual components themselves are not entirely new. The reliance on existing image-to-3D methods can limit the quality of the 3D guidance, especially in cases of occlusion or complex geometry. The method depends on the performance of the video object segmentation, 3D reconstruction, and correspondence finding. If any of these modules fail it will hurt the overall quality of the final edit.

**Significance:**

*   **Strengths:**  The paper addresses a relevant and challenging problem: enabling more intuitive and controllable 3D-aware image editing. The resulting framework has practical applications in areas like e-commerce and digital content creation. The demonstrated qualitative results show significant improvements in handling complex 3D transformations compared to existing methods. The quantitative evaluations using LPIPS and FID support these observations. Fast inference speed of the presented method is important as the method relies on real-world application.
*   **Weaknesses:** The reliance on relatively clean videos of objects with significant movements might limit the method's applicability to all scenarios. The paper lacks a thorough analysis of the limitations of the approach, especially in cases where the initial 3D reconstruction is poor or where the video data is noisy. The paper does not explore the effect of different video datasets. Generalization capabilities to novel object classes are not clear.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of image editing. The key strengths include the novel data generation pipeline, the integration of 3D priors, and the demonstration of improved performance on challenging 3D editing tasks. However, the reliance on existing techniques and potential limitations related to data quality and generalization slightly reduce the overall impact. A score of 7 reflects the paper's significant contribution while acknowledging the areas where further research and improvements could be made.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10
